Table 11:
Self-consistent order paramter symmetry-breaking strengths, calculated at
\kappa_{\Gamma}=\kappa_{K}=0
and
U_{0}=0
.
The projector ansatz at
\nu=0
and
\nu=-2
have
B[O^{f}]=\sqrt{2}=1.414
and
B[O^{f}]=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}=1.225
, respectively, and the symmetry-breaking strengths in
O^{c}
and
O^{cf}
are assumed to be subsidiary and negligibly weak.
The self-consistent values verify this picture.

The procedures of our self-consistent Hartree-Fock calculation closely follow Ref.
[
37
]
. The only modification is that
\hat{H}_{\rm A,H}
are added. We briefly summarize the main steps and numeric parameters.

The Hamiltonian used in the numeric calculations includes the kinetic energy
\hat{H}_{0}
(
Eq.

26
), the full Coulomb interactions
\hat{H}_{\rm C}=\hat{H}_{U}+\hat{H}_{W}+\hat{H}_{J}+\hat{H}_{V}
(
Eqs.

27
,
28
,
29
,
30
and
31
), and the on-
f
-site splittings
\hat{H}_{\rm A,H}
, which are induced by phonons and the atomic Hubbard. The physical parameters we adopt have been presented in the main text.
Interactions are decomposed with the following mean-field order parameters,

\displaystyle O^{f}_{\beta\eta s,\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathrm{MBZ}}\langle f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}\beta\eta s}f_{\mathbf{k}\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\rangle

(184)

\displaystyle O^{c}_{b\eta s,b^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathrm{MBZ}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}\langle c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}b\eta s}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}b^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}

(185)

\displaystyle O^{cf}_{b\eta s,\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathrm{MBZ}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}}\langle c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}b\eta s}f_{\mathbf{k}\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\rangle

(186)

Here, we have divided
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
that runs over the whole momentum space satisfying
|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|\lesssim\Lambda_{c}
into
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}=\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{G}
, where
\mathbf{k}\in\mathrm{MBZ}
, and
|\mathbf{G}|\lesssim\Lambda_{c}
is a moiré reciprocal lattice vector.
Eq.

184
is equivalent to
Eq.

79
, in the absence of translation symmetry breaking. The
\mathbf{k}
-dependence is averaged, which is validated by the absence of
\mathbf{k}
-dependence in the interacting terms
\hat{H}_{\rm C}
and
\hat{H}_{\rm A,H}
.

In the self-consistent loops, we have only kept the
|\mathbf{G}|=0
shell, because
c
-electrons with large
\mathbf{G}
quickly decouple from the low-energy theory. The convergence is tested by further including the
|\mathbf{G}|\leq\sqrt{3}k_{\theta}
shells.
When plotting the mean-field bands in
Fig.

4
, the
|\mathbf{G}|\leq\sqrt{3}k_{\theta}
shell is exploited to capture the correct periodicity in the moiré reciprocal space.
A
13\times 13

\mathbf{k}
mesh in the MBZ is taken during the self-consistent loops.

In the main text, we have assumed that the symmetry-breaking effect is mainly carried by
O^{f}
, so that the phonon-favored orders are mainly selected by the on-
f
-site
\hat{H}_{\rm H,A}
, by perturbatively lowering the corresponding total energies.
Now we quantitatively verify this statement.
First, we define the
b,b^{\prime}=1,2
and
b,b^{\prime}=3,4
blocks in
O^{c}
as
O^{c,12}
and
O^{c,34}
, respectively, and define the
b=1,2
,
b^{\prime}=3,4
block as
O^{c,1234}
. We also define the
b=1,2
and
b=3,4
blocks in
O^{cf}
as
O^{cf,12}
and
O^{cf,34}
, respectively.
Without spontaneously breaking the crytalline
C_{2z}T
,
C_{3z}
,
C_{2x}
, the time-reversal
T
, and valley U(1) and the spin SU(2), the above order parameters, which are all
8\times 8
matrices, can only contain the following components

\displaystyle O^{f}|_{\rm sym}

\displaystyle\propto\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}s^{0}

(187)

\displaystyle O^{c,12}|_{\rm sym}

\displaystyle\propto\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}s^{0},\qquad O^{c,34}|_{\rm sym}\propto\sigma^{0,x}\tau^{0}s^{0},\qquad O^{c,1234}|_{\rm sym}=0

(188)

\displaystyle O^{cf,12}|_{\rm sym}

\displaystyle\propto\sigma^{0}\tau^{0}s^{0},\qquad O^{cf,34}|_{\rm sym}=0

(189)

We therefore quantify the symmetry-breaking strength of an
8\times 8
matrix
O
through

\displaystyle B[O]=\bigg|\bigg|O-O|_{\rm sym}\bigg|\bigg|

(190)

where
||\cdots||
is the Frobenius norm. We tabulate the typical symmetry-breaking strengths found in the self-consistent calculations in
Table

11
. It can be seen that, the
O^{f}
sector carries the major symmetry-breaking effect, and the self-consistent strength is close to the projector ansatz proposed in the main text. The symmetry-breaking in
O^{c}
and
O^{cf}
sectors, on the other hand, are much weaker. The results thus validate the perturbative analysis of the phonon-favored order presented in the main text using the projector ansatz of
O^{f}
.

In general, phonons also induce
cf
and
cc
interactions, which have not been incorporated in our current calculations. Since the Bloch function spread of
c
-electrons are
0.22\sim 0.33a_{M}

[
94
]
, similar to or even wider than the
f
-electron Wannier orbitals (
0.18\sim 0.19a_{M}
, see
Section

II.2
), the corresponding
cf
and
cc
strength will be of the same order, or weaker than, the
f
-on-site interactions
\hat{H}_{\rm A,H}
.
However, due to the much weaker symmetry-breaking strength in the
O^{c}
and
O^{cf}
parameters, the
cf
and
cc
interaction will contribute much less to the total Hartree-Fock energy than the on-site
\hat{H}_{\rm A,H}
.
To be concrete, the Hartree-Fock energy correction due to
\hat{H}_{\rm A}
is proportional to
B[O^{f}]^{2}
, which is of the
O(1)
order. However, the correction due to
c^{\dagger}f^{\dagger}fc
interactions will be proportional to
B[O^{f}]B[O^{c}]
and
B[O^{cf}]^{2}
, and the correction due to
c^{\dagger}c^{\dagger}cc
will be proportional to
B[O^{c}]^{2}
, which are both much smaller than
O(1)
.
Therefore, further incorporating these effects should not qualitatively affect the results presented in the main text.

Appendix H
EPC vertices from the microscopic tight-binding lattice

In this section, we will start from the microscopic lattice model of MATBG to derive the continuum model, including the static electron Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{0}
(summarized in
Section

II.1
), and the EPC vertices
\hat{H}_{\rm epc}
(summarized as the
C^{(\chi)}(\mathbf{q})
matrices in
Section

III.2
). The hopping amplitudes between two carbon
p_{z}
-orbitals that are spatially separated by
\mathbf{r}
is modeled by the standard Slater-Koster form given in Eq. (
3
). In general, depending on whether the electron hops within one layer or across two layers, we divide both
\hat{H}_{0}
and
\hat{H}_{\rm epc}
into the single-layer part (subscript SLG) and the inter-layer part (subscript moiré).

The physical results will be presented in the coordinate system where
\mathbf{K}^{(2)}-\mathbf{K}^{(1)}
lies exactly along the
y
-axis (which we term as the MATBG coordinates, exemplified in
Fig.

1
) in the end. Nevertheless, while deriving the SLG hoppings, it is more convenient to start with a coordinate system where
\mathbf{K}^{(l)}
lies exactly along the
x^{\prime}
-axis (which we term as the SLG coordinates, and differs by an angle of
\pm\frac{\theta}{2}
from the MATBG coordinates), and then rotate the results back to the MATBG coordinates. We will thus adopt this convention, and when the SLG coordinates are used, the layer index
l
will be kept implicit, too.

H.1
Single-layer hoppings

In the SLG coordinates, we denote the directions of the three nearest neighbor bonds (pointing from
A
sublattice to
B
sublattice) as
\mathbf{d}_{1,2,3}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}_{1}=\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{3}}(0,1,0),~~\mathbf{d}_{2}=\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{3}}(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},-\frac{1}{2},0),~~\mathbf{d}_{3}=\frac{a_{0}}{\sqrt{3}}(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2},-\frac{1}{2},0)

(191)

Correspondingly, the graphene
\mathbf{K}
-valley is located at
\mathbf{K}=\frac{4\pi}{3a_{0}}(1,0,0)
.

As the hopping amplitude decays exponentially with the atom spacing, it suffices to keep the nearest-neighbor hoppings. We check the nearest-neighbor hopping between the
B
sublattice and
A
, which we expand to the linear order of lattice vibration
\mathbf{u}_{i}
,

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{d}_{j}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}+\mathbf{d}_{j})-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}))\approx t(\mathbf{d}_{j})+\left(\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}+\mathbf{d}_{j})-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)})\right)\cdot\nabla t(\mathbf{d}_{j})

(192)

By observing that

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{d}_{j})=V_{\pi},~~~~\nabla t(\mathbf{d}_{j})=-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}

(193)

and carrying out the inverse Fourier transformation of
Eq.

33
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(l,\alpha)})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}}\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(l,\alpha)}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(l,\alpha)}}

(194)

we obtain

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{d}_{j}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}+\mathbf{d}_{j})-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}))\approx V_{\pi}+\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)}}\left(\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}-\mathbf{u}^{(A)}_{\mathbf{q}}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}_{j}}

(195)

It can be thus seen that the out-of-plane modes will not enter the SLG EPC vertex, as all
\mathbf{d}_{j}
lie in-plane. In terms of the second-quantized operators, the electrons hop within the layer as,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm SLG}=\sum_{s}\sum_{\mathbf{d}_{j}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(A)}}\psi^{\dagger}_{s}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)})~t(\mathbf{d}_{j}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}+\mathbf{d}_{j})-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}))~\psi_{s}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}+\mathbf{d}_{j})+\mathrm{H.c.}

(196)

Substituting the inverse Fourier transformation of Eq. (
1
),

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{s}(\mathbf{R}^{(l,\alpha)})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\eta}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|\lesssim\Lambda}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(l,\alpha)}}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},l,\alpha,\eta,s}

(197)

and Eq. (
195
) into
\hat{H}_{\rm SLG}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm SLG}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{g}}\sum_{s}\sum_{{\mathbf{R}}^{(A)}}\sum_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|\lesssim\Lambda}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)}}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},A,\eta,s}\times

(198)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{d}_{j}}\left[V_{\pi}+\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)}}\left(\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(A)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}\right]\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},B,\eta^{\prime},s}e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K})\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{(A)}+\mathbf{d}_{j})}+\mathrm{H.c.}

Depending on containing
\mathbf{u}_{i}
or not, we separate
\hat{H}_{\rm SLG}=\hat{H}_{\rm 0,SLG}+\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG}
. For
\hat{H}_{\rm 0,SLG}
, summation over
\mathbf{R}^{(A)}
imposes momentum conservation,
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}
and
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
, therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm 0,SLG}=

\displaystyle V_{\pi}\sum_{s}\sum_{\eta}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},A,\eta,s}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}+\mathrm{H.c.}

(199)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}a_{0}V_{\pi}\right)\sum_{s}\sum_{\eta}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},A,\eta,s}\left[\eta k_{x}-ik_{y}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}+\mathrm{H.c.}

where we have used that
e^{i\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}
, and then expanded
e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}
to the first order in
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}
. For
\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG}
, summation over
\mathbf{R}^{(A)}
requires
\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+(\eta-\eta^{\prime})\mathbf{K}
,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\sum_{s}\sum_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|\lesssim\Lambda}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}}\delta_{\mathbf{q},\ \mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+(\eta-\eta^{\prime})\mathbf{K}}

(200)

\displaystyle\times\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},A,\eta,s}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta^{\prime}\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\left(\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(A)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},B,\eta^{\prime},s}+\mathrm{H.c.}

Depending on whether the phonon is
\Gamma
-phonon or the
K
-phonon, the momentum conservation will impose
\eta=\eta
and
\overline{\eta}=\eta^{\prime}
, respectively. We will thus further separate
\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG}=\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG,\Gamma}+\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG,K}
.

H.1.1
\hat{H}_{\rm 0,SLG}

For
\hat{H}_{\rm 0,SLG}
, Eq. (
199
), by defining
v_{F}=-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}V_{\pi}a_{0}
, and adding up the two parts that are Hermitian conjugate to each other, we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}=v_{F}\sum_{s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda}\sum_{\eta}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}\left[k_{x}\sigma_{x}\tau_{z}+k_{y}\sigma_{y}\tau_{0}\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{\mathbf{k},\alpha,\eta,s}

(201)

where we have introduced the Pauli matrices
[\sigma_{\mu}]_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}
in the sublattice index, and
[\tau_{\mu}]_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}
in the valley index, with
\mu=0,x,y,z
.

Now we recover the layer index and rotate back to the MATBG coordinates. In that way, we should replace
(k_{x},k_{y})
with
(\cos\frac{\theta}{2}k_{x}+(-1)^{l}\sin\frac{\theta}{2}k_{y},\sin\frac{\theta}{2}k_{x}+(-1)^{l-1}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}k_{y})
for layer
l=1,2
, respectively, so that the Dirac equations from the two layers are slightly rotated relative to each other.
Nevertheless, it has been shown that dropping all the
O(\theta)
terms have little effect on the electronic properties
[
36
]
. Therefore, we will adopt this approximation, writing
\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}
as

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}=v_{F}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|\lesssim\Lambda}\sum_{\eta,l,s}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}\left[k_{x}\sigma_{x}\tau_{z}+k_{y}\sigma_{y}\tau_{0}\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{\mathbf{k},l,\alpha,\eta,s}

(202)

Written in terms of the continuum fields Eq. (
2
), Eq. (
202
) reads,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}=v_{F}\sum_{\eta,l,s}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}(\mathbf{r})\left[-i\partial_{x}\sigma_{x}\tau_{z}-i\partial_{y}\sigma_{y}\tau_{0}\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})

(203)

H.1.2
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},\Gamma}

For the
\Gamma
-phonon term in Eq. (
200
), we focus on those
\mathbf{u}^{(\alpha)}_{\mathbf{q}}
with
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}\ll 1
which impose
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
. Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc},\mathrm{SLG},\Gamma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},A,\eta,s}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\left(\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(A)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}+\mathrm{H.c.}

(204)

First, expanding
e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}
to the linear order of
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(A)}\right)\approx\left(\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(B)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(A)}\right)+(i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j})\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(B)}\right)

(205)

Moreover, here it suffices to approximate
e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}\approx 1
, because the zeroth order will not get canceled in any phonon channel, leaving all higher orders negligible.
Referring to the definition of the
\Gamma
-phonon modes, Eq. (
34
), the square bracket in Eq. (
204
) will now read,

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\left(\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(B)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(A)}\right)+(i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j})\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(B)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}\right]

(206)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}+u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\right]+\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4}\right)(iq_{x}-\eta q_{y})(\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q},x}+i\eta\mathbf{u}^{(B)}_{\mathbf{q},y})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}+u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\right]+\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\left(-iq_{x}+\eta q_{y}\right)\left(u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XA}}+i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YA}}\right)

Note that in deriving the last line, the EPC strength of the second term is linear in
|\mathbf{q}|
. Consequently, if decomposing
\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(B)}
into acoustic and optical components, only the acoustic component will contribute significantly, because the acoustic phonon frequency is also linear in
|\mathbf{q}|
.
The optical component can be simply added to the first term, where the linear-in-
|\mathbf{q}|
strength will be negligible.
By inserting Eq. (
206
) into the square brackets of Eq. (
204
), and writing down the Hermitian conjugate terms explicitly,
\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG,\Gamma}
reads

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc},\mathrm{SLG},\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\times\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}

(207)

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},A,\eta,s}\left[\left(-\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}+u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\right]+\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\left(-iq_{x}+\eta q_{y}\right)\left(u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XA}}+i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YA}}\right)\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}\left[\left(-\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[-i\eta u_{-\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}+u_{-\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\right]+\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\left(iq_{x}+\eta q_{y}\right)\left(u_{-\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XA}}-i\eta u_{-\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YA}}\right)\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},A,\eta,s}

where we have used
u_{\mathbf{q},\chi}^{\dagger}=u_{-\mathbf{q},\chi}
. Next, by first changing the summed variables as
\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{k}
and then
-\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}
,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc},\mathrm{SLG},\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\times\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|<\Lambda}

(208)

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},A,\eta,s}\left[\left(-\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}+u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\right]+\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\left(-iq_{x}+\eta q_{y}\right)\left(u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XA}}+i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YA}}\right)\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},B,\eta,s}\left[\left(-\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[-i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}+u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\right]+\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\left(-iq_{x}-\eta q_{y}\right)\left(u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XA}}-i\eta u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YA}}\right)\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},A,\eta,s}

Using the Pauli matrix
[\sigma_{\mu}]_{\alpha^{\prime},\alpha}
and
[\tau_{\mu}]_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}
,
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}
further simplifies to

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc},\mathrm{SLG},\Gamma}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{\sqrt{3}a_{0}}{4\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,\eta^{\prime},s}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}\left[u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XA}}\left[(iq_{x})(-\sigma_{x}\tau_{0})+(iq_{y})(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z})\right]\right.

(209)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\left.u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YA}}\left[(iq_{x})(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z})+(iq_{y})(\sigma_{x}\tau_{0})\right]\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{\mathbf{k},\alpha,\eta,s}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,\eta^{\prime},s}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}\left[u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{XO}}\left(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z}\right)-u_{\mathbf{q},\mathrm{YO}}\left(\sigma_{x}\tau_{0}\right)\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{\mathbf{k},\alpha,\eta,s}

Now we recover the layer index
l
, and rotate Eq. (
209
) back to the MATBG coordinates. In doing so, not only
\mathbf{k}
and
\mathbf{q}
need to be rotated by an angle of
\pm\frac{\theta}{2}
, the
\mathrm{XA}
and
\mathrm{XO}
modes will also mix with
\mathrm{YA}
and
\mathrm{YO}
by an amount of
\sim\frac{\theta}{2}
.
Nonetheless, the extra correction terms to appear are all of the order
O(\theta)
, and hence negligible.
Fouriered to the continuum fields by exploiting Eq. (
2
) and (
39
), and by replacing
v_{F}=-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}a_{0}V_{\pi}
,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},\Gamma}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{v_{F}}{r_{0}}\right)\sum_{\eta,l,s}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}(\mathbf{r})\left[\left(\partial_{y}u_{l,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r})-\partial_{x}u_{l,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})\right)(\sigma_{x}\tau_{0})+\right.

(210)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~\left.\left(\partial_{y}u_{l,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})+\partial_{x}u_{l,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r})\right)(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z})\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left(\sqrt{6}\frac{v_{F}}{a_{0}r_{0}}\right)\sum_{\eta,l,s}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}(\mathbf{r})\left[u_{l,\mathrm{XO}}(\mathbf{r})\left(\sigma_{y}\tau_{z}\right)-u_{l,\mathrm{YO}}(\mathbf{r})\left(\sigma_{x}\tau_{0}\right)\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})

Note that, if defining

\displaystyle\left[\mathbf{A}^{(l)}(\mathbf{r})\right]_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\frac{1}{r_{0}}\left(\partial_{x}u_{l,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})-\partial_{y}u_{l,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r}),-\partial_{y}u_{l,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})-\partial_{x}u_{l,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r})\right)\otimes[\tau_{z}]_{\eta^{\prime},\eta}

(211)

then the acoustic part in
\hat{H}_{\rm epc,SLG,\Gamma}
modifies
\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}
into the form of a Dirac fermion coupled to a pseudo gauge field,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}+\hat{H}^{(ac)}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}=v_{F}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}(\mathbf{r})\left[\left(-i\partial_{x}-\mathbf{A}^{(l)}_{x}(\mathbf{r})\right)\sigma_{x}\tau_{z}+\left(-i\partial_{y}-\mathbf{A}^{(l)}_{y}(\mathbf{r})\right)\sigma_{y}\tau_{0}\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})

(212)

which takes the form as derived in Ref.
[
95
,
90
]
. On the other hand, if identifying that
\sqrt{6}\frac{v_{F}}{a_{0}r_{0}}=\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{|V_{\pi}|}{r_{0}}\approx 145\mathrm{eV\cdot nm^{-1}}
, then the optical EPC vertex along with the corresponding EPC strengths (the second term in Eq. (
210
) involving the
\mathrm{XO}
and
\mathrm{YO}
branches) will take the same form as that used in Ref.
[
77
,
61
]
.

H.1.3
\hat{H}_{{\rm epc,SLG},K}

We then investigate the
K
-phonons involved in Eq. (
200
). For this sake, we focus on those
\mathbf{u}^{(\alpha)}_{\mathbf{q}}
with
\mathbf{q}\approx\eta\mathbf{K}
, which impose
\eta^{\prime}=\overline{\eta}
. To begin with, we exploit the SLG coordinates and re-define
\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}+\eta\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, so that for the re-defined
\mathbf{q}
, there is
\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}\ll 1
.

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},K}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\sum_{\eta,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}

(213)

\displaystyle\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},A,\overline{\eta},s}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}}^{(B)}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}}^{(A)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},B,\eta,s}+\mathrm{H.c.}

We still start with the expression inside the square bracket.
Even simpler than the case of
\Gamma
-phonons, here it suffices to directly approximate both
e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}\approx 1
and
e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}\approx 1
, because all the in-plane
K
-phonons have finite frequencies (
\gtrsim 100
meV), thus any EPC elements linear in
|\mathbf{q}|
can be ignored.
Thereof, we obtain

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{i\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}}^{(B)}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{d}_{j}}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}}^{(A)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}\right]

(214)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}}^{(B)}e^{-i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}}^{(A)}e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\right)\cdot\hat{\mathbf{d}}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)\left[(-i\eta)\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K},x}^{(A)}+\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K},x}^{(B)}\right)-\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K},y}^{(A)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K},y}^{(B)}\right)\right]

To compare this expression with the definition of
K
-phonon modes, Eq. (
37
), we first recover the layer index, and rotate it back to the MATBG coordinates, which generates negligible
O(\theta)
corrections. Then we exploit
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\mathbf{K}^{(l)}-\mathbf{K}_{l}
, and write
\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}=\mathbf{q}+\eta\boldsymbol{\Gamma}^{\prime}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3}{2}\right)\left[(-i\eta)\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)},x}^{(A)}+\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)},x}^{(B)}\right)-\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)},y}^{(A)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)},y}^{(B)}\right)\right]=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\left[u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{A1}}-i\eta u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{B1}}\right]

(215)

By inserting Eq. (
215
) into Eq. (
213
), and then adding up the two layers,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},K}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,s,l}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},l,A,\overline{\eta},s}\left[u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{A1}}-i\eta u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{B1}}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},l,B,\eta,s}

(216)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,s,l}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},l,B,{\eta},s}\left[u_{-\mathbf{q}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{A1}}+i\eta u_{-\mathbf{q}-\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{B1}}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},l,A,\overline{\eta},s}

where we again exploited
u_{\mathbf{q},l,\chi}^{\dagger}=u_{-\mathbf{q},l,\chi}
for
\chi@\mathbf{K}\in\{\mathrm{A1},\mathrm{B1}\}
. we have also written out the Hermitian conjugate terms explicitly. By a relabeling of the summed variables,
\overline{\eta}\to\eta
,
\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{k}
, and then
-\mathbf{q}\to\mathbf{q}
,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},K}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,l,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},l,A,\overline{\eta},s}\left[u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{A1}}-i\eta u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{B1}}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},l,B,\eta,s}

(217)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,l,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},l,B,\overline{\eta},s}\left[u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{A1}}-i\eta u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{B1}}\right]\psi_{\mathbf{k},l,A,\eta,s}

Using the Pauli’s
\sigma_{\mu}
and
\tau_{\mu}
, we obtain,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},K}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(-\frac{V_{\pi}}{r_{0}}\right)\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}\right)\sum_{\eta,l,s}\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}\left[u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{A1}}\left(\sigma_{x}\tau_{x}\right)-u_{\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}_{l},l,\mathrm{B1}}\left(\sigma_{x}\tau_{y}\right)\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{\mathbf{k},l,\alpha,\eta,s}

(218)

Fouriered to the real-space continuum with Eq. (
2
) and (
39
), and replacing
v_{F}=-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}a_{0}V_{\pi}
,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},K}=\left(\sqrt{6}\frac{v_{F}}{a_{0}r_{0}}\right)\sum_{\eta,s,l}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}(\mathbf{r})\left[u_{l,\mathrm{A1}}(\mathbf{r})\left(\sigma_{x}\tau_{x}\right)-u_{l,\mathrm{B1}}(\mathbf{r})\left(\sigma_{x}\tau_{y}\right)\right]_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime},\alpha\eta}\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})e^{-i\eta\mathbf{K}_{l}\cdot\mathbf{r}}

(219)

Under the gauge choice
Eqs.

9
and
38
, the above expressions are translationally invariant. By re-choosing the gauge for electron fields in accordance with the phonon fields,
Eq.

219
will take an identical form to that used in Ref.
[
77
,
61
]
.

Using the SLG coordinates, lattice vibration corresponding to the phonon
u_{0,\mathrm{A1}}
takes the real-space pattern,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)})=u_{0,\mathrm{A1}}(-\sin\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)},-\cos\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)}),\quad\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(B)})=u_{0,\mathrm{A1}}(-\sin\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(B)},\cos\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(B)})

(220)

while lattice vibration corresponding to the phonon
u_{0,\mathrm{B1}}
takes the real-space pattern,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(A)})=u_{0,\mathrm{B1}}(\cos\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)},-\sin\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(A)}),\quad\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(B)})=u_{0,\mathrm{B1}}(\cos\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(B)},\sin\mathbf{K}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(B)})

(221)

It can thus be verified that, both modes change the nearest-neighboring bond lengths, thus induce EPC.

H.2
Inter-layer moiré couplings

The inter-layer tunnelings are given by,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm Moire}=\sum_{s}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\psi_{s}^{\dagger}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})t(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}))\psi_{s}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})+\mathrm{H.c.}

(222)

By projecting to the low-energy Dirac electrons with Eq. (
1
), there is

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm Moire}=\sum_{s}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},1,\alpha,\eta,s}\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{u}}\psi_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},s}+\mathrm{H.c.}

(223)

with

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{u}}=\frac{1}{N_{g}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}t(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}))e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}

(224)

We can separate
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}+\mathbf{u}_{z}
into the in-plane components and the out-of-plane component, hence

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}))

\displaystyle=t(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}+\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}-\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}))

(225)

\displaystyle+\partial_{z}t(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})\left[\mathbf{u}_{z}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{u}_{z}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})\right]

To proceed, we divide the 3D parameter space of
\mathbf{r}
into multiple 2D
\mathbf{r}_{x}
-
\mathbf{r}_{y}
planes labeled by
\mathbf{r}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{z}}
. Within each plane,
t(\mathbf{r})
can be Fouriered as (with
\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}=\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}_{x}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{r}_{y}
),

\displaystyle t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\mathbf{r}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{z}})}=\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~e^{-i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{r}}~t(\mathbf{r}),\qquad\qquad t(\mathbf{r})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{r}}~t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\mathbf{r}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{z}})}

(226)

Note that
\mathbf{p}
can run over the entire 2D momentum space. Correspondingly,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\mathbf{r}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{z}})}=\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~e^{-i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{r}}~\partial_{z}t(\mathbf{r}),\qquad\qquad\partial_{z}t(\mathbf{r})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{r}}~\partial_{z}t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(\mathbf{r}\cdot\hat{\mathbf{z}})}

(227)

Consequently,

\displaystyle t(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}+\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}-\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}))

(228)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{2,\alpha^{\prime}})}e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}))}+\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{2,\alpha^{\prime}})}\left[\mathbf{u}_{z}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{u}_{z}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})\right]

\displaystyle\approx\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~\left[t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}+t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}\left[i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}(\mathbf{R}^{2,\alpha^{\prime}}))\right]+\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}\left[\mathbf{u}_{z}(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)})-\mathbf{u}_{z}(\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})\right]\right]e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{2,\alpha^{\prime}})}

where we have used the fact that
(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})\cdot\hat{{\mathbf{z}}}=c_{0}
is the layer spacing for arbitrary
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
, and then approximated the expression to the first order of
\mathbf{u}_{\parallel}
and
\mathbf{u}_{z}
.
The three terms in the line expression of
Eq.

228
describe the moiré coupling with no vibration, with in-plane vibration, and with out-of-plane vibration, respectively. Inserting them back to
Eq.

224
, they separate the inter-layer matrix element into three terms,

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{u}}=\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{0}+\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\parallel}+\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{z}

(229)

which give rise to the moiré coupling in the continuum model
\hat{H}_{\rm 0,Moire}
, and the in-plane and out-of-plane EPC vertices,
\hat{H}_{\rm epc,Moire,\parallel}+\hat{H}_{\rm epc,Moire,z}
. We now examine each term separately.

H.2.1
\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{Moire}}

Inserting the non-vibrating term (the first term) of
Eq.

228
into
Eq.

224
, there is

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{0}=\frac{1}{N_{g}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\right]\left[\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\right]

(230)

so that
\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}
and
\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}
are decoupled and thus can be evaluated independently. The following identity will be of great use,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{g}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(l,\alpha)}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(l,\alpha)}}=\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(l)}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}+\mathbf{G}^{(l)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(l)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(l,\alpha)}}

(231)

where
\mathbf{G}^{(l)}
is the graphene reciprocal lattice vector of layer
l
, and
\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(l,\alpha)}
denotes position of the
\alpha
-sublattice modulo graphene lattice vectors
\mathbf{R}^{(l)}
. Recall that in our scenario,
|\mathbf{k}|\ll|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|
, and
\mathbf{p}
can run over the entire 2D momentum space. With these knowledge, by further discretizing the integral
\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\to\frac{1}{N_{g}\Omega_{g}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}}
(where
\Omega_{g}
denotes the area of a graphene unit cell), we obtain,

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{0}=\sum_{\mathbf{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}\frac{t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}}{\Omega_{g}}e^{i\mathbf{G}^{(1)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(2)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}+\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}+\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}

(232)

Since
t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}
will decay fast with
|\mathbf{p}|
, it suffices to keep the shortest
\mathbf{p}
’s. If
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
, as
|\mathbf{k}|,|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|\ll|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|
, dictated by the
\delta
-factors, the shortest possible
\mathbf{p}
is reached by three terms, namely those satisfying
\mathbf{p}\approx\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}+\mathbf{G}^{(l)}=\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}
for
j=1,2,3
.
For such terms, since
\left|\frac{1}{t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}}\frac{\partial t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}}{\partial\mathbf{p}}\right|\sim\left|\frac{1}{t(\mathbf{r})}\frac{\partial t(\mathbf{r})}{\partial\mathbf{r}}\right|^{-1}\sim r_{0}
, and the typical deviation of
\mathbf{p}
from
\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}
can be estimated as
|\mathbf{p}-\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|\sim|\mathbf{k}|\sim|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|\sim k_{\theta}\ll|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|
, and
k_{\theta}r_{0}\ll 1
, therefore, we can approximate
\frac{t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}}{\Omega_{g}}\approx\frac{t^{(c_{0})}_{\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}}}{\Omega_{g}}=w_{1}\approx 112
meV.
Such an approximation is equivalent to neglecting non-local inter-layer tunnelings.
These three terms will correspond to the scattering processes that satisfy
\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}^{\prime}=\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\left(\mathbf{K}^{(2)}-\mathbf{K}^{(1)}\right)=\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}
(see Fig.
1
), respectively. Moreover, we define and calculate

\displaystyle\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=e^{i\mathbf{G}^{(1)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(2)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}=\begin{pmatrix}1&e^{-i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\\
e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}&1\\
\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},~~~~\mathrm{if}~\mathbf{G}^{(l)}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}=\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}

(233)

On the other hand, if
\overline{\eta}=\eta^{\prime}
, to match the
\delta
-signs, the length of the shortest
\mathbf{G}^{(l)}
, and therefore, the shortest
\mathbf{p}
, can be estimated as
|\mathbf{p}|\sim|\mathbf{G}^{(l)}|\sim\frac{|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|}{\theta}
, rendering the corresponding
t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}
vanishingly small. Thereby, it suffices to keep the intra-layer elements,

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{0}\approx\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\sum_{j}w_{1}\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}}

(234)

Combined with its Hermitian conjugate, we write down the second-quantized form of
\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{Moire}}
,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{Moire}}=w_{1}\sum_{\eta}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j},1,\alpha,\eta}\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{k},2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta}+\mathrm{H.c.}

(235)

which when inverse-Fouriered to the continuum real-space with
Eq.

2
reads,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{Moire}}=w_{1}\sum_{\eta}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta}(\mathbf{r})\left[T(\mathbf{r})\right]_{l\alpha\eta,l^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}\psi_{l^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(236)

where
T(\mathbf{r})
is a layer-off-diagonal Hertimian matrix defined as
\left[T(\mathbf{r})\right]_{1\alpha\eta,2\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}=\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}[T^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
, and
T^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})=\sum_{j=1}^{3}e^{i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{r}}[T_{j,\eta}]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
(namely,
Eq.

6
),

It can be verified that, at the AA-stacked regions (
e^{i(\mathbf{q}_{j+1}-\mathbf{q}_{j})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{AA}}=1
), only diagonal
[T(\mathbf{r}_{AA})]_{A\eta,A\eta}
and
[T_{\eta}(\mathbf{r}_{AA})]_{B\eta,B\eta}
survive, while at the AB-stacked (
e^{i(\mathbf{q}_{j+1}-\mathbf{q}_{j})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{AB}}=e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}}
) or the BA-stacked (
e^{i(\mathbf{q}_{j+1}-\mathbf{q}_{j})\cdot\mathbf{r}_{BA}}=e^{-i\frac{2\pi}{3}}
) regions, only off-diagonal
[T_{\eta}(\mathbf{r}_{AB})]_{A\eta,B\eta}
or
[T_{\eta}(\mathbf{r}_{BA})]_{B\eta,A\eta}
survive. Phenomenologically, to account for the lattice corrugation that diminishes the area of the AA-stacked regions and increases the AB/BA-stacked regions, we will introduce a numeric factor
u_{0}
to the diagonal elements, as

\displaystyle\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\begin{pmatrix}u_{0}&e^{-i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}\\
e^{i\eta\frac{2\pi}{3}(j-1)}&u_{0}\\
\end{pmatrix}_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}

(237)

We take
u_{0}\approx 0.8
for
\theta\approx 1.05^{\circ}
.

H.2.2
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire,\parallel}}

By inserting the in-plane vibrating term of
Eq.

228
into
Eq.

224
, and Fouriering the phonon fields using
Eq.

33
, we get

\displaystyle~\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\parallel}

(238)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{g}^{3/2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\times\left(t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}\right)e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})}\times

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~i\mathbf{p}\cdot\left(\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}^{(1)}}\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(1,\alpha)}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}-\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}^{(2)}}\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\right)\times e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{g}^{3/2}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}^{(1)}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(1,\alpha)}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\right]\left[\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\right]

\displaystyle~~~-\frac{1}{N_{g}^{3/2}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}^{(2)}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\right]\left[\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\right]

Still,
\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}
and
\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}
are decoupled, and Eq. (
231
) can be applied to each summation. We obtain,

\displaystyle~\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\parallel}

(239)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}(i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(1,\alpha)})\frac{t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}}{\Omega_{g}}e^{i\mathbf{G}^{(1)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(2)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}+\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}+\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}(i\mathbf{p}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})\frac{t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}}{\Omega_{g}}e^{i\mathbf{G}^{(1)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(2)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}+\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}+\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}

For
\boldsymbol{\Gamma}
-phonons, namely those
\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}
with
|\mathbf{q}|\ll|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|
, the analysis parallels that of
\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{Moire}}
, namely only three terms that satisfy
\mathbf{G}^{(l)}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}=\eta C^{j-1}_{3z}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}
contribute, and they correspond to scatterings that satisfy
\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}
. For these terms, still, we treat
\frac{t_{\mathbf{p}}^{(c_{0})}}{\Omega_{g}}\approx\frac{t^{(c_{0})}_{\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}}}{\Omega_{g}}=w_{1}
; and moreover, by observing that
\mathbf{p}\approx\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(l)}\approx\frac{\eta}{\theta}\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\mathbf{q}_{j}
up to
O(k_{\theta})
momenta, we get

\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\parallel}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(w_{1}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{3}i\eta\left(\frac{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\mathbf{q}_{j}}{\theta}\right)\cdot\left(\mathbf{u}^{(1,\alpha)}_{\mathbf{q}}-\mathbf{u}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}_{\mathbf{q}}\right)\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}}[T_{j,\eta}]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}},~~\mathrm{if}~|\mathbf{q}|\ll|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|

(240)

By comparing to
Eqs.

34
and
35
, such an EPC vertex will involve the
(r,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA})
modes, as well as both
(c,\mathrm{XO}/\mathrm{YO})
and
(r,\mathrm{XO}/\mathrm{YO})
modes, but does not involve the
(c,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA})
modes. It can be quickly checked that, the EPC strengths to the
(l,\mathrm{XO}/\mathrm{YO})
modes are
\frac{w_{1}|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|}{v_{F}/a_{0}r_{0}}\sim\frac{1}{30}
times weaker than that in
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}
(Eq. (
210
)), hence negligible.

For
\mathbf{K}
-phonons, namely those
\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}
with
\mathbf{q}\approx\eta\mathbf{K}^{(l)}
, necessarily
\overline{\eta}=\eta^{\prime}
. If we check the
\langle 1,\alpha,\overline{\eta},\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{\parallel}
element, then for the
\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(1,\alpha)}
terms, the shortest
\mathbf{p}
is reached by the following three terms,
\mathbf{p}\approx\eta\mathbf{K}^{(2)}+\mathbf{G}^{(2)}=\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}
, or equivalently,
\mathbf{p}\approx\mathbf{G}^{(1)}-2\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}=\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(1)}
; while for the
\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q}}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}
terms, the shortest
\mathbf{p}
is reached by the following three terms,
\mathbf{p}\approx-\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}+\mathbf{G}^{(1)}=-\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(1)}
, or equivalently,
\mathbf{p}\approx\mathbf{G}^{(2)}+2\eta\mathbf{K}^{(2)}=-\eta C_{3z}^{j-1}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}
. For all these cases,
\frac{t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}}{\Omega_{g}}\approx w_{1}
as well. Consequently, the EPC strengths to the
\mathbf{K}
-phonons will also be
\frac{w_{1}|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|}{v_{F}/a_{0}r_{0}}\sim\frac{1}{30}
times weaker than that contained in
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,SLG},\mathbf{K}}
(Eq. (
219
)). We also remark that
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire}}
will couple to more
\mathbf{K}
-phonon modes other than
(l,\mathrm{A1})
and
(l,\mathrm{B1})
. Nevertheless, due to their weak coupling strengths, we will neglect them altogether.

Exploiting the definition of
u_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{XA}}
and
u_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{YA}}
as given in
Eqs.

34
and
35
, we get

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire,\parallel}}=\frac{w_{1}}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j},1,\alpha,\eta}i\eta\left[\left(\frac{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\mathbf{q}_{j}}{\theta}\right)_{x}u_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{XA}}+\left(\frac{\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\mathbf{q}_{j}}{\theta}\right)_{y}u_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{YA}}\right]\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{k},2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta}+\mathrm{H.c.}

(241)

Next we write it in the continuum real-space with
Eqs.

39
and
2
, as

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire,\parallel}}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{w_{1}}{\theta}\right)\sum_{\eta}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{1,\alpha,\eta}(\mathbf{r})\left[\left(\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\sum_{j=1}^{3}(i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j})e^{i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\right)_{x}u_{r,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})\right.

(242)

\displaystyle~~~~~~\left.+\left(\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\sum_{j=1}^{3}(i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j})e^{i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\right)_{y}u_{r,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r})\right]\psi_{2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta}(\mathbf{r})+\mathrm{H.c.}

By noting that
\left[\partial_{\mathbf{r}}T^{(\eta)}(\mathbf{r})\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}=\sum_{j=1}^{3}(i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j})e^{i\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\left[T_{j,\eta}\right]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}
, and defining
u_{r}(\mathbf{r})=(u_{r,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r}),u_{r,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r}))
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire,\parallel}}=\left(\frac{w_{1}}{\theta}\right)\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta}(\mathbf{r})\left[u_{r}(\mathbf{r})\cdot\left(\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times\partial_{\mathbf{r}}T(\mathbf{r})\right)\right]_{l\alpha\eta,l^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}\psi_{l^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(243)

Equivalently, by writing the first-quantized form of
\hat{H}_{0}
as
\left[H(\mathbf{r})\right]_{l\alpha\eta,l^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}
,
\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire}}
can be written as

\hat{H}_{\mathrm{epc,Moire,\parallel}}=\left(\frac{1}{\theta}\right)\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}\ \psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta}(\mathbf{r})\left[u_{r}(\mathbf{r})\cdot\left(\hat{\mathbf{z}}\times[\partial_{\mathbf{r}},H(\mathbf{r})]\right)\right]_{l\alpha\eta,l^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}\psi_{l^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(244)

where
H(\mathbf{r})
is the first quantized form contained in
\hat{H}_{0}=\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{SLG}}+\hat{H}_{0,\mathrm{Moire}}
.

H.2.3
\hat{H}_{\rm epc,Moire,z}

Inserting the out-of-plane vibrating term of
Eq.

228
into
Eq.

224
, and exploiting the Fourier transformation
Eq.

33
,

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{z}

(245)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{g}^{3/2}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{p}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i(\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}\times\left(\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}\right)e^{i\mathbf{p}\cdot(\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})})}\times

\displaystyle~\qquad\qquad\left[\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}^{(1)}}\mathbf{u}^{(1,\alpha)}_{\mathbf{q},z}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(1,\alpha)}}-\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathrm{gBZ}^{(2)}}\mathbf{u}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}_{\mathbf{q},z}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\right]\times e^{i(\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}-\mathbf{p})\cdot\mathbf{R}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}

By noting that
Eq.

245
takes the identical form as (the first line of)
Eq.

238
, a similar derivation will follow. We arrive at

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{z}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q},z}^{(1,\alpha)}\frac{\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}}{\Omega_{g}}e^{i\mathbf{G}^{(1)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(2)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}+\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}+\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}

(246)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\sum_{\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q},z}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}\frac{\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}}{\Omega_{g}}e^{i\mathbf{G}^{(1)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(1,\alpha)}}e^{-i\mathbf{G}^{(2)}\cdot\boldsymbol{\tau}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}+\eta\mathbf{K}^{(1)}+\mathbf{G}^{(1)}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}+\eta^{\prime}\mathbf{K}^{(2)}+\mathbf{G}^{(2)}}

We check the intra-valley processes (
\eta=\eta^{\prime}
, related to
\Gamma
-phonons) first.
Still, it suffices to keep the shortest
\mathbf{p}
, which satisfies
|\mathbf{p}|\approx|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|
. Correspondingly, we approximate
\frac{\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{p}}}{\Omega_{g}}\approx\frac{\partial_{z}t^{(c_{0})}_{\mathbf{K}^{(l)}}}{\Omega_{g}}=-1.11\times\frac{w_{1}}{r_{0}}
. By a similar derivation to
Section

H.2.2
,

\displaystyle\langle 1,\alpha,\eta,\mathbf{k}|\hat{H}|2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\rangle_{z}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\left(\frac{-1.11w_{1}}{r_{0}}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{3}\left(\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q},z}^{(1,\alpha)}-\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{q},z}^{(2,\alpha^{\prime})}\right)\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j}}[T_{j,\eta}]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}

(247)

Comparing to
Eqs.

34
and
35
,
Eq.

247
can decompose to
(r,\mathrm{ZA})
phonons as well as
(c/r,\mathrm{ZO})
phonons, with equal overall EPC strengths. However, as detailed in
Section

III.1
,
\omega_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{ZA}}=11
meV while
\omega_{\mathbf{q},c/r,\mathrm{ZO}}=100
meV. Correspondingly, the effective electron interactions mediated by
(c/r,\mathrm{ZO})
phonons will be
\left(\frac{\omega_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{ZA}}}{\omega_{\mathbf{q},c/r,\mathrm{ZO})}}\right)^{2}\sim\frac{1}{81}
times weaker than
(r,\mathrm{ZA})
. As a result, it suffices to keep the
(r,\mathrm{ZA})
component in
Eq.

247
. For inter-valley processes (
\eta=\overline{\eta^{\prime}}
, related to
K
-phonons), the shortest
\mathbf{p}
satisfies
|\mathbf{p}|\approx|\mathbf{K}^{(l)}|
as well, resulting in an overall EPC strength
\frac{w_{1}}{r_{0}}
, the same as the intra-valley processes. Since all the
K
-phonon frequencies are much higher than
\omega_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{ZA}}
, the corresponding matrix elements can be dropped as well. Summing up, we obtain the EPC vertex induced by out-of-plane phonons as

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm epc,Moire,z}=\frac{-1.11w_{1}/r_{0}}{\sqrt{N_{g}}}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\psi^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}+\eta\mathbf{q}_{j},1,\alpha,\eta}u_{\mathbf{q},r,\mathrm{ZA}}[T_{j,\eta}]_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{k},2,\alpha^{\prime},\eta}+\mathrm{H.c.}

(248)

Note that the EPC vertex is irrelevant of
\mathbf{q}
, and takes the same form as the inter-valley coupling matrix in
\hat{H}_{\rm 0,Moire}
. Written in terms of the continuum fields,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm epc,Moire,z}=-1.11\frac{w_{1}}{r_{0}}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta}(\mathbf{r})u_{r,\mathrm{ZA}}(\mathbf{r})[{T}(\mathbf{r})]_{l\alpha\eta,l^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}\psi_{l^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})

(249)

H.3
Summary

The EPC vertex
\hat{H}_{\rm epc}
in the continuum model contains
Eqs.

210
,
219
,
243
and
249
, and for a quick index based on phonon modes, please refer to
Table

12
.

To convert them to the form displayed in the main text, one first needs to Fourier transform the continuum phonon fields
u_{\chi}(\mathbf{r})
to
u_{\mathbf{q},\chi}
based on
Eq.

39
. Then, we note that
Eq.

210
has been written in the layer basis
(l,\chi_{g}@\Gamma)
with
l=1,2
, hence one needs to convert the layer basis to the co-moving/relative basis
(c/r,\chi_{g}@\Gamma)
using
Eq.

35
. Obtaining the
C
-matrices presented in the main text (
Eq.

45
to
Eq.

56
) will be direct.

Appendix I
EPC vertices from deformation potential

In this section, we also discuss EPC vertices arising from the deformation potential
[
95
,
96
,
97
,
98
]
.
By inducing a local area modulation proportional to
\partial_{x}u_{l,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})+\partial_{y}u_{l,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r})
, the (longitudinal) deformation of a single-layer graphene creates a variation in the background positive charge density, hence a potential to the electrons in the same layer,

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{\rm epc,deform}=g_{1}\sum_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})\left[\partial_{x}u_{l,\mathrm{XA}}(\mathbf{r})+\partial_{y}u_{l,\mathrm{YA}}(\mathbf{r})\right]\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})

(250)

Note that
Eq.

250
is a mechanism different from the tight-binding hopping variation analyzed in
Appendix

H
.

Eq.

250
induces intra-layer scatterings that are
diagonal
in the sublattice index
\alpha
.
The strength of
g_{1}
has been estimated to be in the range of
g_{1}=16\sim 30
eV
[
95
,
96
,
97
,
98
]
.
We now show that, for an
f
-site,
Eq.

252
simply contributes a negative on-site Hubbard that can be absorbed to the much larger Coulomb
U
, and does not affect the multiplet splitting, nor the selection of symmetry-breaking correlated states.

Using
Eqs.

39
and
35
, we can write
Eq.

250
in the form of
Eq.

43
, so as to obtain the corresponding
C
-matrices. We find (where the superscript
d
is introduced to emphasize the deformation potential origin)

\displaystyle C^{(c,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA}),d}(\mathbf{q};\mathbf{r})=g_{1}\frac{(i\mathbf{q}_{x/y})}{\sqrt{2}}\rho_{0},\qquad\quad C^{(r,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA}),d}(\mathbf{q};\mathbf{r})=g_{1}\frac{(i\mathbf{q}_{x/y})}{\sqrt{2}}\rho_{z}

(251)

Crucially, for the relative modes,
C^{(r,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA}),d}(\mathbf{q};\mathbf{r})
anti-commute with the emergent symmetry
\widetilde{S}=\rho_{y}\sigma_{z}
defined in
Section

IV.1.1
.
Therefore, similar to the tight-binding EPC vertices of
(c,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA}/\mathrm{XO}/\mathrm{YO})
(see
Section

IV.1.1
), the relative in-plane acoustic modes will have vanishing coupling to the
f
-orbitals through the deformation potential mechanism. It thus suffices to examine the form and strength induced by the co-moving modes.

Since
C^{(c,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA}),d}(\mathbf{q};\mathbf{r})
are proportional to identity in the
l,\alpha,\tau,s
indices, the deformation potential simply couples the co-moving phonon modes to the total electron density
\sum_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}\psi^{\dagger}_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})\psi_{l,\alpha,\eta,s}(\mathbf{r})
, which highly resembles the Coulomb interaction
Eq.

7
.
The projected EPC vertex thus reads

\displaystyle M^{(c,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA}),d}_{\beta\eta,\beta^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle=g_{1}\cdot\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{(i\mathbf{q}_{x/y})}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\sum_{l,\alpha}\left[w^{(\eta)*}_{l\alpha,\beta}(\mathbf{r})~w^{(\eta)}_{l\alpha,\beta^{\prime}}(\mathbf{r})\right]

(252)

\displaystyle=g_{1}\cdot\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{(i\mathbf{q}_{x/y})}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\int\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{r}~e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{r}}\sum_{l,\alpha}\left[w^{(\eta)*}_{l\alpha,\beta}(\mathbf{r})~w^{(\eta)}_{l\alpha,\beta}(\mathbf{r})\right]

\displaystyle=g_{1}\cdot\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{(i\mathbf{q}_{x/y})}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\left[\alpha_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}\lambda_{1}^{2}}{4}}+\alpha_{2}^{2}e^{-\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}\lambda_{2}^{2}}{4}}\left(1-\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{4}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\approx g_{1}\cdot\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}\cdot\frac{(i\mathbf{q}_{x/y})}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot e^{-\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}}{4}}

where the integral can be understood as a projected density form factor. Below we first explain the results in
Eq.

252
.

The 2nd line of
Eq.

252
derives from the following fact: as analyzed in Ref.
[
37
]
, due to the
C_{2z}T
and
P
symmetries, the density form factors, hence
Eq.

252
, must be proportional to
\delta_{\beta,\beta^{\prime}}
.
To be concrete,
C_{2z}T
(
Eq.

25
) and
P
(
Eq.

64
) dictate that

\displaystyle w^{(+)*}_{l\alpha,\beta}(\mathbf{r})=w^{(+)}_{l\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(-\mathbf{r})=(-i)(-1)^{\beta}(-1)^{l}w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})

(253)

Therefore, for the square bracket in
Eq.

252
, if
\beta^{\prime}=\overline{\beta}
, there will be

\displaystyle\sum_{l,\alpha}\left[w^{(+)*}_{l\alpha,\beta}(\mathbf{r})~w^{(+)}_{l\alpha,\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})\right]

\displaystyle=(-i)(-1)^{\beta}\sum_{l,\alpha}\left[(-1)^{l}w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(+)}_{l\alpha,\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})\right]

(254)

\displaystyle=(-i)(-1)^{\beta}\sum_{l}\left[(-1)^{l}w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(+)}_{l\alpha,\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})+(-1)^{l}w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(+)}_{l\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})\right]

\displaystyle=(-i)(-1)^{\beta}\sum_{l}\left[(-1)^{l}w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(+)}_{l\alpha,\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})-(-1)^{l}w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})w^{(+)}_{l{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})\right]=0

where we first explicitly wrote out the summation over
\alpha
, and then swapped the positions
w^{(+)}_{\overline{l}\alpha,\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})
and
w^{(+)}_{l\overline{\alpha},\overline{\beta}}(\mathbf{r})
, and finally changed the dummy variable
l\to\overline{l}
.

To obtain the 3rd line in
Eq.

252
, we explicitly calculated the density form factor using the Gaussian fits
Eqs.

23
and
24
, and to obtain the 4th line in
Eq.

252
, we approximated the density form factor using one Gaussian packet that approaches
1
as
\mathbf{q}\to 0
, and spans a width
\lambda^{\prime}
as an average of
\lambda_{1,2}
.

Using
Eq.

252
, we can calculate the zero-frequency interaction, which thus takes a density-density form as the on-site Hubbard
U
.
The frequency dependence can be argued as un-important due to the slow kinetic energy of the
f
-electrons, similar to the tight-binding hopping variation mechanism. In terms of definition
Eq.

73
, the effective interaction induced by
Eq.

252
reads

\displaystyle V^{\eta^{\prime}\eta}_{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime},\beta\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\delta_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}}{N_{g}m_{0}}\sum_{\chi=(c,\mathrm{XA}/\mathrm{YA})}\sum_{|\mathbf{q}|\lesssim\Lambda}\frac{M^{(\chi),d}_{\beta\eta^{\prime},\beta\eta^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{q})M^{(\chi),d}_{\alpha\eta,\alpha\eta}(\mathbf{q})}{v^{2}_{s}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}

(255)

\displaystyle=-\delta_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\cdot\frac{(\frac{g_{1}}{v_{s}})^{2}}{2m_{0}}\cdot\theta^{2}\Omega_{M}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}~e^{-\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{2}}{2}}=-\delta_{\beta^{\prime},\beta}\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\cdot\frac{(\frac{g_{1}}{v_{s}})^{2}}{2m_{0}}\cdot 1.35\times 10^{-3}

In the last line, we have replaced
\frac{1}{N_{g}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\to\Omega_{g}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}\mathbf{q}}{(2\pi)^{2}}
.
If assuming
g_{1}=19
eV
[
96
]
,
V^{\eta^{\prime}\eta}_{\beta\alpha,\beta\alpha}=-6.75
meV.
In terms of the symmetry-parametrized form
Eqs.

75
and
76
, one can verify that
Eq.

255
does
not
contribute to any multiplet-splitting parameters
J_{\rm a,b,d,e}
, but only to a Hubbard
J_{\rm c}
that can be absorbed to the repulsive
U
.
